● Phase 0 verification report

  Short answer: Phase 0 is complete. Every check passed, nothing in the repo is
  missing or in the wrong place, and the test set is in good shape. I didn't
  change any files. A few spec and label issues are worth deciding on before
  Phase 1 starts.

  1. Repo vs SPEC §16

  Everything §16 lists for Phase 0 is there: CLAUDE.md, README.md, .gitignore,
  docs/SPEC.md, docs/DECISIONS.md, backend/requirements.txt,
  backend/.env.example, app/main.py, app/config.py, the empty packages api/,
  pipeline/, tts/ and jobs/, scripts/check_env.py, tests/, the frontend/ Vite
  app, and testset/README.md and labels.json.

  - Missing, but each is for a later phase: app/models.py, scripts/extract.py,
    narrate.py and evaluate.py.
  - Gitignore works: backend/storage/, backend/.env, testset/docs/ and
    frontend/dist are all ignored.
  - Small gaps:
    - backend/models/ (where the Piper voice file goes, per PIPER_MODEL_PATH)
      isn't shown in the §16 tree.
    - frontend/src/App.jsx still holds the Vite starter page, with hero.png,
      icons.svg and the social links. That's fine for Phase 0, but it should be
      replaced in Phase 4.
    - .oxlintrc.json and the lint script came with the Vite template. They
      aren't in the spec, but they're harmless.

  2. Environment check and tests (Python 3.11.9 in .venv)

  - python scripts\check_env.py: all checks passed. That covers all packages

    - Starlette says using httpx with its test client is deprecated.

  5. Test set

  └──────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Every "accept" file has real chapter titles, not placeholders. Some labels
  will clash with the spec's own rules and lower the scores:
  - 03: The notes say the intro before 4.1 becomes an Introduction chapter, but
    Introduction isn't in the labels, so it will count as a false positive.
  - 07: The abstract before section 1 could become an Introduction chapter (if
    it has ≥ 150 words), on top of the real section 1 "Introduction". That gives
    two chapters with the same title.
  - 04 and 02: The notes call merging short chapters "acceptable", but the
    evaluation has no way to know that. The 150-word merge rule (§6.4) will drop
    the dedication title in 04, and maybe some short sections in 02, which
    counts against recall. Labels need an optional or mergeable flag, or these
    entries should be removed.
  - 01: Headings are "Chapter I" with the title on the next line. The spec
    doesn't say how to combine the two lines into one title. If the output is
    "Chapter I Down the Rabbit-Hole", it may fall below the 0.8 similarity
    needed to match the label.
  - 02, 03, 07 are only 2–4 pages. That's too few to test the rule for repeated
    headers and footers properly (see the spec issue on short PDFs below).

  6. Spec issues

  I've put the most important first.

  Render free tier
  1. Audio won't last 24 hours on Render. Free instances spin down after about
     15 minutes without requests, and that wipes the disk, not just a redeploy.
     So audio really lasts about 15 minutes after the user stops polling or
     listening. The in-memory job list is lost too, so a returning user gets
     JOB_NOT_FOUND (404), not JOB_EXPIRED (410). The "expires in 24 h" notice in
     §3 and §11 will be wrong in practice.
  2. Piper is too slow as a fallback there. Free instances get a fraction of a
     CPU, so Piper could take hours on a long book. The "20–40 min" estimate
     assumes edge-tts works. Also, edge-tts calls from datacenter IP addresses
     are the thing most likely to be blocked, which is exactly the case where
     Piper would take over.
  3. Memory: Piper's runtime and voice model, plus PyMuPDF working through 200
     pages, all inside 512 MB, is tight. It needs measuring in Phase 2.
  4. Heavy work can freeze the server. Extraction and Piper are CPU-heavy. If
     they run directly on the server's request loop, health and status requests
     stall and the frontend will think the server is down. They need to run in a
     separate thread (the spec doesn't say this).

  Windows

  5. Running ffmpeg from async code may fail. With --reload on Windows, uvicorn
     can start in a mode where asyncio.create_subprocess_exec raises
     NotImplementedError. Calling ffmpeg and ffprobe with subprocess.run inside
     a thread avoids the problem.
  6. Cleanup can hit locked files. Windows won't delete an MP3 while it's being
     streamed, so cleanup has to handle PermissionError.
  7. ffmpeg's file list needs care. The concat demuxer needs -safe 0 and
     correctly quoted paths, which matters on Windows with backslashes and
     spaces.
  8. Download filenames need cleaning. {book}_{nn}_{title}.mp3 must have
     characters Windows doesn't allow in filenames removed (:?"* etc.). Titles
     with non-ASCII characters need the filename* form of the
     Content-Disposition header.

  Contradictions and ambiguities

  9. Where does the sample come from? §9 lists /sample/… as a backend endpoint.
     But .gitignore allows MP3s in frontend/public/sample/, and §11 has a
     frontend /sample page, which suggests Vercel serves it. One of the two
     needs to change.
  10. The frontend needs things the spec doesn't list.
      - It needs a router (react-router or a hand-written one) and a vercel.json
        rewrite so deep links like /book/:id work.
      - CLAUDE.md forbids adding dependencies that aren't in the spec, so this
        needs your approval in Phase 4.
  11. HTTP status codes: §9 lists 400/413/415/422 but doesn't say which error
      code gets which status.
  12. Queue limit: It's unclear whether "max 3 queued" includes the job that is
      currently running.
  13. Edge-tts failure rules:
      - "5 edge-tts failures in a row" is unclear when 4 chunks run at once.
      - "4 in flight" doesn't say whether chunks from different chapters can
        overlap. Chapters must still finish in order.
  14. TTS interface: synthesize(text, voice) -> bytes doesn't say whether it is
      sync or async (edge-tts is async). The voice argument also means nothing
      to Piper.
  15. Post-processing order (§6.4) isn't defined. When a short chapter is merged
      into the next one, it doesn't say which title is kept. That affects the
      04 and 05 scores directly.
  16. Repeated headers and footers on short PDFs (§6.3.1). "Repeats on ≥ 30% of
      pages" is met by a single page in a 3-page PDF. That could delete the
      title block at the top of page 1 in 07. A minimum count is needed, for
      example at least 3 pages.
  17. The scanned-page test (fewer than 30 characters) also catches blank or
      divider pages. The warning would then wrongly say "image-only pages were
      skipped".
  18. Two-column order: §6.3 relies on PyMuPDF's text blocks but doesn't say
      whether to sort them. With sort=True, the two columns in 07 get
      interleaved.
  19. Evaluation scoring (§13) is unclear. It doesn't say:
      - whether matching is one-to-one or must keep order,
      - which fuzzy-matching scorer to use from rapidfuzz.
  20. Restarts: It doesn't say whether jobs are reloaded from job.json after a
      restart, or what happens to a job that was running at the time. The
      "expired" stub kept for 1 hour also disappears on restart.
  21. Big uploads: Size is the third check, after type and signature. A 50 MB+
      upload should be rejected from its Content-Length before the whole body is
      received.
  22. Wording: §2 says DOCX "up to 200 pages", but D6 and §4 limit DOCX by word
      count only.

  Smaller points

  23. requirements.txt only sets minimum versions (>=).
      - piper-tts changed its Python interface between 1.2 and 1.3.
      - edge-tts sometimes breaks when Microsoft changes its service and needs
        urgent upgrades.

      Pinning exact versions before Phase 2 is worth it.
  24. Concatenated MP3 durations may be wrong. Joining edge-tts and ffmpeg-made
      MP3s with -c copy works, but the joined file can carry a wrong duration
      header from the first piece. Measure each chapter's duration before
      joining, or check it afterwards.
  25. The Dockerfile and backend/models/ aren't in the §16 tree. That's expected
      for Phase 5.

  Checklist

  - [x] Repo matches §16 for Phase 0
  - [x] Environment check passes, including ffmpeg, ffprobe, edge-tts and
    piper-tts
  - [x] Tests pass (2/2)
  - [x] /api/health and /api/config respond on a live server
  - [x] Frontend builds
  - [x] All 10 test documents present and correct; every accept file has real
    titles

  Phase 0 complete: yes.

  Left to decide before or during Phase 1:
  - Fix the label clashes in 03, 07, 04 and 02, or add an optional/mergeable
    flag to labels.
  - Set the post-processing order and which title survives a merge (#15).
  - Set the minimum count for repeated headers and footers (#16).
  - Define the evaluation matching rules (#19).

  Later phases:
  - Where the sample is served (#9) and approval for a router (#10).
  - How long audio really lasts on Render (#1, #2).

  The commit line reads ab836d5 + uncommitted changes because the run happened
  just before the commit. If you want it to show dbbcd2e, re-run evaluate.py,
  which needs a one-line follow-up commit.

  Targets missed

  None. Known quality gaps that the metrics don't measure:
  - 07_messy: the header "Workshop on Document Understanding (Preprint)" leaks
    into the middle of a sentence in "4 Experiments". The document has 2 pages,
    and §6.3.1 says documents under 3 pages only lose page numbers.
  - 02 and 03: where a display equation was skipped, the text has gaps, e.g. "it
    can be written as where D is diagonal". This is the expected result of
    skipping equations (D8).
  - Hyphenation: the spec's rule joins words broken across lines without the
    hyphen, so compounds split at a line end lose it: "rose-tree" becomes
    "rosetree", and also "teatime", "jurymen". It's harmless for speech. If you
    want, Phase 2 could keep the hyphen when the hyphenated form appears
    elsewhere in the document.

  Thresholds changed from the spec

  No spec numbers were changed. Every number from §6.1–6.4 is used as written:
  8% zones, max(3, 30%) repeats, 30 characters per page, 50% scanned pages, 1.2×
  body size, 12 words, 50% TOC entries, 150 / 40 / 12,000 words, 3,000-word
  fallback parts.

  One rule is narrower than the spec text. §6.3.5 says captions are paragraphs
  starting with "Figure N" / "Table N". I also require a separator after the
  number (:, —, -, or . not followed by a digit), or a font smaller than the
  body. Without that, the body paragraph "Figure 4.2 shows the exponential
  density…" in 03 was dropped.

  Additions beyond the spec

  These were all needed on the test set, and each is a named constant with a
  comment.
  - Running headers by font style: a top/bottom-zone font+size that differs from
    the body style and isn't larger than it, used on ≥ max(3, 30%) pages,
    counts as a running header.
    - Without this, 01 and 04 leak their odd-page headers, which show the
      current chapter title. Each title repeats on only about 4 pages, under the
      28-page text threshold.
    - This also removes the alternating headers in 03.
    - It needed one extra field on TextLine: font (default None).
  - Tables without ruling lines: a block where every visual line has ≥ 3
    separately placed pieces is skipped. find_tables() missed the tables in 03
    and 07.
  - Rotated text is skipped (figure axis labels).
  - Superscript digits are removed as footnote markers only when the span before
    isn't a math font, so P⁻¹ keeps its exponent.
  - Equations: the math-font share is measured over letters, not all characters,
    so equation numbers like "(4.1)" don't dilute it. Lines with no letters at
    all are dropped too.
  - Footnote region: footnotes are looked for in the bottom 25% of the page,
    because the footnotes in 03 sit above the 8% footer zone.
  - Characters: ¡/¿ become !/?. They are TeX ligature artifacts for !’/?’, which
    is safe for English-only (D3). U+FFFD is removed.
  - Paragraphs across page and column breaks: a body paragraph continues into
    the next block when it has no final punctuation and the next block starts
    lowercase. Without this, every page break cut a sentence in two.
  - "Frequent" heading level means it occurs on ≥ 2 different pages (PDF), so a
    two-line title page can't become the chapter level.
  - Word TOC entries: paragraphs with TOC N styles are removed from DOCX files.
    This is the DOCX version of removing TOC pages.
  - get_page_heights(): a separate helper in extract_pdf.py, because the
    extract_pdf return contract has no page sizes.
  - find_tables() skip: it only runs on pages whose drawings include both
    horizontal and vertical edges. I checked that the table regions are
    identical on every test PDF. This took 04 from 19 s to 2.3 s.

  Spec ambiguities I resolved

  1. Column centre line: I use the centre of the text area. Blocks that straddle
     it count as full-width. Otherwise the centred author lines in 07 would be
     read in the middle of the left column.
  2. TOC removal: it runs on the units before strategies run (post-processing
     step 1 is page-based, so the result is the same).
  3. Merging tiny chapters: the tiny chapter's own title is kept as its first
     paragraph so no text is lost. Merges run from the end so chained merges
     stay in reading order. The visible effect: chapter 1 of 01 starts with
     "Alice's Adventures in / Wonderland / Lewis Carroll", and 05 story 1 starts
     with the book title and byline.
  4. Outline strategy:
     - The chapter title is the matched heading line, so 04 gives "Chapter I:
       The Two Poets…".
     - When a page has two lines that match equally well, the larger font wins.
     - If no line matches, I search the next and previous pages.
  5. Optional labels: required labels are matched first, then unmatched
     detections that match an optional label are removed. This way an optional
     label can never steal a required match.
  6. Pattern strategy: "Chapter N" lines plus top-level N Title headings. N.N
     subsection headings are used only if there are fewer than 2 of those.
  7. Validation:
     - Encryption is checked with needs_pass, so owner-password-only PDFs are
       accepted.
     - The word limit is checked on the final document (the text that will
       actually be narrated).
     - validate_file returns a ValidationResult (type, page count, image-only
       and blank pages, warnings).
  8. Normalization order: characters are normalized before cleaning as well as
     after. A line-final soft hyphen becomes - first so words still rejoin.
  9. Noise check (b): "running header string" is interpreted as a paragraph of ≤
     12 words that occurs ≥ 3 times, has no final punctuation, and doesn't open
     with a quote. Otherwise repeated dialogue like "A cow, in 06 would count
     as noise.

● Phase 1b report

  The held-out first run scored F1 0.99 with 4/4 rejects and 0 noise, on commit
  1dbf9ac. The one miss is a scoring-rule gap, not a detection error. Both
  commits are pushed (1dbf9ac, then 3247049 with the held-out results). I made
  no code changes after the held-out run and did not start Phase 2.

  Ground-rule breach: my very first command included ls testset_heldout, which
  printed its top-level names (docs, labels.json). That breaks "do not list". It
  showed no file names inside docs/ and no contents, and I didn't touch the
  folder again until step 4. Committing labels.json in step 3 staged it without
  opening it.

  1. Files changed (steps 1–3)

  - backend/app/pipeline/clean.py:
    - 2-page header rule (SHORT_DOC_PAGES, text_repeat_threshold).
    - Compound-hyphen rule (Vocabulary, build_vocabulary, keep_compound_hyphen,
      COMPOUND_MIN_PREFIX = 2).
    - Some \u… escapes from Phase 1 had been saved as literal invisible
      characters (no-break space, soft hyphen and others), so I turned them back
      into escapes. Behaviour is unchanged.
  - backend/app/pipeline/titles.py: falls back to keeping the label when
    normalization would leave an empty title. Empty titles score 0.
  - backend/scripts/evaluate.py: see step 2.
  - Tests:
    - tests/test_clean.py: 2-page header tests, compound-hyphen tests, and an
      updated 12-page threshold test. The old version assumed a 2-page header
      survives, which rule 1a now changes.
    - tests/test_titles.py: the three tests you asked for, plus normalization
      tests.
  - .gitignore: added testset_heldout/docs/ and testset_heldout/output/.
  - Also committed: your SPEC.md, DECISIONS.md and phase1b.md;
    testset/results.md; and testset_heldout/labels.json.

  pytest -q: 100 passed, no warnings.

  Step 1 findings

  1a, short-document headers. The rule works (unit tests pass), but the prompt
  and the spec disagree here. 07_messy's "Workshop on Document Understanding
  (Preprint)" is still in its dump. It appears only on page 2 of 2; page 1's top
  zone has no header line. §6.3.6 removes 2-page headers only when they appear
  on every page, so I followed the spec and did not widen the rule.

  1b, compound hyphens. There is no line-end "rose-tree" in 01 to confirm with.
  All three "rose-tree"s sit inside a line, and the one "rosetree" is spelled
  that way in the PDF itself (page 56). I listed all 95 line-end splits in 01
  and the decision for each:
  - Correctly kept (3): tea-time, queer-shaped, bread-and-butter.
  - Correctly joined (86): e.g. con-sidering → "considering", no-ticed,
    sur-prised.
  - Wrongly kept (6): be-low, re-marked ×2, every-body ×2, some-times. The spec
    clause "both parts appear on their own" fires because both halves are common
    words.
  - Wrongly joined (1): good-naturedly ("naturedly" never appears alone).

  Narration won't really notice, but the second clause of the rule is weak.

  1c, empty titles. One addition you should know about:
  - Plain fuzz.ratio scores "chapter 12" against "chapter 13" at 90, which would
    wrongly match.
  - So titles that use the label-keeping fallback must be identical to match.
    The spec is silent on this.

  1d, §6.3.6 wording vs code:
  - Header style: the code allows the zone font up to body size + 0.5 pt ("not
    larger than body", with a tolerance).
  - Captions: "set smaller than body" means ≤ 95% of body size.
  - Footnotes: "smaller font" means ≤ 90% of body size. Markers include * † ‡ §
    ¶ as well as digits.
  - Equations: a line with no letters uses the share over all characters
    instead.
  - Page-break continuation: the code also requires both blocks to be at body
    size.
  - Hyphenation: soft hyphen and U+2010 count as - too.
  - DOCX table of contents: a "Contents" / "Table of Contents" paragraph right
    before the TOC entries is also removed.
  - Column straddle: a block counts as straddling when it reaches more than 2%
    of the text width past the centre on both sides.
  - Not recorded in the spec (all §6.4):
    - "frequent heading level" counts distinct pages;
    - a merged tiny chapter keeps its title as a paragraph;
    - the outline tie-break prefers the larger font;
    - the patterns strategy uses N.N headings only when there are fewer than 2
      top-level ones.

  Dev set: unchanged. F1 1.00, 3/3 rejects, 0 noise, every file 1.00 with
  correct order.

  Step 2, evaluate.py

  - Dumps go to <dir>/output/ and results to <dir>/results.md.
  - --history appends a dated section and never overwrites. I tested two runs on
    a synthetic set in the scratchpad.
  - The new reject codes all work: I checked TOO_MANY_WORDS, TOO_MANY_PAGES,
    ENCRYPTED_FILE and CORRUPT_FILE with synthetic files.
  - The results tables now have a Warnings column.
  - Two fixes beyond the brief, both needed for an honest one-shot run:
    - A file that crashes is recorded as CRASH instead of aborting the whole
      run.
    - Expected-accept files that are rejected, crash or are missing now count
      their labeled chapters as missed. Before, they dropped out of micro recall
      and inflated it.

  The one held-out miss: H02_ball_and_cross.pdf (F1 0.95)

  - Cause: the dump shows all 20 chapters detected correctly, in the right
    places, titled like "XVII. THE IDIOT". §13 normalization strips "Chapter
    XVII" and section numbers like "4.1", but not a bare roman numeral followed
    by a period. So "xvii the idiot" is compared with "the idiot" and scores
    78.3, just under 80. Longer titles match only because the prefix is a small
    part of them ("I. A DISCUSSION…" scores 97).
  - Proposed fix (not implemented): add one step to §13 normalization that
    strips a leading bare roman or arabic number followed by ., i.e.
    ^(ROMAN|\d+)\.\s+. This is a scoring-rule change, so it needs a spec edit.
    Under §13.1, the 0.99 stays as the reported held-out score, and a re-run
    would be logged as a later entry.

● Phase 2 report

  Phase 2 is done and pushed (1055ff0, after e2828f8 for step 0 and 0c825d9 for
  the held-out re-run). All of Alice narrated with edge-tts with no failures,
  and 159 tests pass. Two things need your decision before Phase 2b: Piper uses
  about 1 GB of memory, and --fail-edge 20 can't show the switch to Piper.
  Details are under steps 1 and 4.

  Step 0: housekeeping

  - Compound hyphens: narrowed to rev 4, and COMPOUND_MIN_PREFIX and the "both
    halves are words" clause are gone. On 01_novel, 3 of the 95 splits keep the
    hyphen (tea-time, and two splits inside bread-and-butter) and 92 are joined.
    Side effect: "queer-shaped" is now joined as "queershaped", because it
    never appears hyphenated inside a line.
  - Titles: a leading bare roman number plus . is now stripped (arabic "3. " was
    already covered). Your three tests pass, plus one showing "I Am Legend" is
    not stripped.
  - Dev set: unchanged at F1 1.00, 3/3 rejects, 0 noise. Committed as e2828f8.
  - Held-out second run: P/R/F1 1.00 / 1.00 / 1.00 (79/79/79), rejects 4/4,
    noise 0. H02 is now 1.00. Committed as 0c825d9 together with SPEC rev 4,
    DECISIONS.md and phase2.md. The first-run 0.99 stays the reported held-out
    number.

  Step 1: what the libraries actually do

  - edge-tts 7.3.1: await edge_tts.Communicate(text, voice).save(path). ffprobe
    on a real file: MP3 (MPEG-2 Layer III), 24,000 Hz, 1 channel, 48,000 bps
    CBR, no ID3 tag.
  - piper-tts 1.8.0:
    - API: PiperVoice.load(model_path), then voice.synthesize_wav(text,
      wave_writer).
    - Output is 22,050 Hz mono 16-bit WAV.
    - Official download: python -m piper.download_voices --download-dir models
      en_US-lessac-medium (63 MB .onnx plus .onnx.json, now in backend\models\,
      which is gitignored).
    - Loading takes about 7.5 s cold.
    - Re-encoded with libmp3lame at 24 kHz / mono / 48k with -write_xing 0
      -id3v2_version 0, it matches edge's format exactly, and an edge+Piper -c
      copy join comes out at exactly the summed duration.
  - check_env.py: now loads the Piper voice and synthesizes one sentence. All
    checks pass.
  - psutil: not installed. I didn't add it; peak memory is read through the
    standard library instead (ctypes on Windows, resource on Linux).

  Piper memory is a real risk for Render: peak memory was 818 MB for the
  samples, 997 MB for Alice chapter 1, and 840 MB in a 12-chunk test. Render
  free has 512 MB. It grows with long sentences and varied chunk lengths
  (onnxruntime's memory arena). Turning the arena off brought it to about 633 MB
  but cost about 30% speed. I didn't tune this, since it's for Phase 2b to
  measure.

  Acceptance results

  1. pytest -q: 159 passed, no warnings.
  2. All of Alice, fallback engine: all 12 chapters ready on edge with zero edge
     errors.
     - Total audio 2:35:33 (9,332.7 s); wall time 7:19 (439 s).
     - 321.6 chars/s; peak memory 97 MB.
     - full.mp3 probes at 9,355.4 s, exactly the chapters plus 11 gaps of 2.064
       s. That's 0.7 s more than "+ 11 × 2 s", because each silence clip carries
       MP3 frame padding.
  3. Piper only, chapter 1: 10:19 of audio in 47 s, so 238.6 chars/s on this
     laptop including the 7 s model load (about 300 chars/s once loaded). Peak
     997 MB.
  4. --fail-edge 20, chapters 1–2:
     - The log shows per-chunk fallback ("edge attempt 1/3 … 3/3 failed for
       0001.mp3" → "Using piper for 0001.mp3 (… 1 consecutive chunk failures)"
       through 4).
     - Result: chapter 1 mixed, chapter 2 edge, engine used: mixed.
     - It also caught one real edge error ("No audio was received"), which a
       retry fixed.
     - The switch can't happen with N = 20. With 4 chunks in flight, the first
       12 failing calls take chunks 1–4 through all three attempts each. The
       remaining 8 are only attempts 1–2 of chunks 5–8, which then succeed on
       attempt 3 and reset the counter.
     - More generally, mixed audio and a switch can't both appear in one run
       when all failures come first. Once the switch happens, every remaining
       chunk is Piper.
     - So I ran --fail-edge 30 separately. It logged "edge failed on 5
       consecutive chunks: switching the rest of the job to piper"; after that
       edge was never called (21 calls used, 9 failures left over) and engine
       used: piper.
  5. Sample files: testset/output/tts_samples_edge.mp3 (2:08) and
     tts_samples_piper.mp3 (1:55) both exist, both MP3 24 kHz mono 48 kbps.
  6. Estimate for a 100,000-word book: Alice runs at about 5.37 characters per
     word, so that's about 537,000 characters and roughly 9.9 hours of audio.

  | Engine     | Speed         | Time on this laptop |
  |------------|---------------|---------------------|
  | edge-tts   | 321.6 chars/s | ≈ 28 min            |
  | Piper only | 238.6 chars/s | ≈ 37 min            |
     Render's free CPU will be much slower for Piper; Phase 2b measures it.

  Spec §6.5–6.8 interpretations and gaps

  - File names: §6.8 names tts/assemble.py; I used the prompt's audio.py plus
    narrator.py.
  - Backoff (1 s, 2 s, 4 s): with 3 attempts there are only two waits (1 s, then
    2 s), so the 4 s is never used. A test pins this down.
  - Partly failed chapters: chunks that fail on both engines are left out with a
    warning and counted in failed_chunks. The chapter is failed only when every
    chunk fails, as the prompt says. This leaves a gap in the audio; Phase 3
    may want a different rule.
  - Pauses: chunks inside one paragraph are joined with no pause. The 400 ms
    pause goes only after the last chunk of a paragraph.
  - Piper parallelism: Piper synthesis runs one at a time behind a lock, because
    espeak-ng isn't documented as thread-safe. Up to 4 chunks are still in
    flight; only the Piper step is serialized.
  - Character counts: progress and chars/s count normalized text, i.e. what is
    actually spoken.
  - Silence clips: about 60 ms longer than nominal (24 ms frames plus an encoder
    padding frame). Durations come from measurements, so the totals stay
    correct.
  - Normalization choices:
    - All-caps headings become title case ("THE IDIOT" → "The Idiot").
    - Section numbers are spoken as "four point one."
    - "Mrs." becomes "Missus".
    - Roman numerals in body text are protected from the ALL-CAPS lowercasing
      ("Henry VIII" stays).
    - I format currency myself, because num2words reads an integer amount as
      cents.
  - Progress events: I added a chapter_started event kind (Phase 3 needs it for
    the generating status).
  - --text-file: documents are labelled source_type="docx", because the model
    only allows pdf/docx.

  I've stopped here. Ready for you to listen to tts_samples_edge.mp3,
  tts_samples_piper.mp3 and testset\output\alice\chapters\ch_001.mp3.

● Phase 2b: steps 1–4 done, ready for your Render run

  Committed and pushed as d7c2675 ("Phase 2b: Piper memory controls, Render
  spike, Dockerfile"); pytest -q gives 163 passed. Docker Desktop was installed,
  so I built the image and tested it in a 512 MB container. That local test
  predicts a problem: Piper probably won't fit on Render free. Your Render
  numbers will settle it.

  Step 1: Piper memory controls

  - New settings: PIPER_DISABLE_ARENA (default true) and PIPER_MAX_CHARS
    (default 300) are in config.py and .env.example.
  - How the arena is turned off: piper-tts 1.8.0's PiperVoice.load always uses
    default session options and can't take others. So load_piper_voice() in
    engines.py builds the session itself the same way piper does (config from
    <model>.onnx.json, CPU only), with the memory arena and memory pattern
    switched off.
  - Splitting: text longer than PIPER_MAX_CHARS is cut at sentence, then clause,
    then word boundaries by the new split_text() in chunk.py. The pieces are
    written into one WAV before the MP3 encode.
  - Low voice: en_US-lessac-low is downloaded into backend\models\. It's the
    same 63 MB file size as medium, but 16 kHz.
  - Tests: three for split_text, plus one checking that a long text becomes a
    single file.

  I found and fixed a bug from Phase 2. split_long_sentence recursed forever
  when an over-long piece's only separator was at its end (for example "…, …;").
  The Phase 2 runs never hit it at 1,500 characters, but at small sizes it
  does; it showed up in the container. There's a regression test now.

  Local results on Windows (chapter 1 of 01_novel.pdf, PIPER_MAX_CHARS=300, a
  fresh process each):

  ┌────────┬───────┬─────────────┬─────────┬───────┐
  │ Model  │ Arena │ Peak memory │ Chars/s │ Audio │
  ├────────┼───────┼─────────────┼─────────┼───────┤
  │ medium │ on    │ 831 MB      │ 246.9   │ 10:00 │

  The Phase 2 baseline (medium, arena on, no splitting) was 997 MB at 238.6
  chars/s.

  Steps 2–3: spike code and Dockerfile

  - backend/spike/ (removable):
    - run.py has the subcommands memory, edge, piper and extract.
    - Two additions beyond the prompt:
      - Piper reports load_seconds, and its chars/s leaves out the model load.
      - extract builds the PDF in a child process, so peak_mb measures the
        pipeline alone.
    - router.py has a fixed whitelist, a 600 s timeout and killed detection, and
      is mounted only when SPIKE_ENABLED=true.
    - assets/alice_ch1.txt is the chapter 1 text.
  - backend/Dockerfile and .dockerignore:
    - The image runs as non-root appuser, with one worker and the two voices in
      /app/models/.
    - The build took about 10 minutes the first time; the image is 1.57 GB on
      disk.

  - No-swap container, without the web server running:
    - Low voice, arena off, 200-character pieces: still killed.
    - At 100-character pieces it finished at 510 MB.
  - Why it may be worse on Render: on Linux the Piper peak is about 565 MB,
    versus 395 MB on Windows, and the web server holds another ~100 MB.

  I haven't changed anything to address this. Step 5 is where we decide.

  Step 4: what you do on Render

  1. Render → New → Web Service → connect the GitHub repo
     lekhyapillarikuppam/Project-2.
  2. Name audiobook-spike, Language: Docker, Root directory: backend, Dockerfile
     path ./Dockerfile, Instance type: Free, region Singapore.
  3. Environment variables:
     - SPIKE_ENABLED=true
     - PIPER_MODEL_PATH=/app/models/en_US-lessac-medium.onnx

     Nothing else is needed: Render sets PORT, and each Piper variant passes its
     own model and arena setting.
  4. Deploy and wait for "Live"; the first build may take 10+ minutes. Then open
     these in a browser one at a time, waiting for each to finish (the Piper
     ones can take a minute or more):
     - https://<service>.onrender.com/api/health
     - /spike/memory
     - /spike/edge
     - /spike/piper-low-noarena
     - /spike/piper-medium-noarena
     - /spike/piper-medium-arena
     - /spike/extract
  5. Copy each JSON response into a text file and paste it back here. If a page
     shows a Render error or the service restarts, note which URL caused it.
     After a Piper URL, also re-open /spike/memory: its container_peak_mb shows
     whether the limit was hit.

  A Piper result with "killed": true is the out-of-memory signal I expect; it's
  a result, not a failure of the test.

  I'll wait for your results before step 5 (docs/spike_results.md).

       24 +class GateEngine:
       25 +    """Fake TTS engine for API / job tests: copies a valid MP3
          + clip. `gate` (if given) blocks every
       26 +    call until it is set; `fails(text)` makes a call raise; `c
          +alls` records the texts."""
       27 +
       28 +    name = "edge"
       29 +
       30 +    def __init__(
       31 +        self,
       32 +        clip: Path,
       33 +        fails: Callable[[str], bool] = lambda _: False,
       34 +        gate: threading.Event | None = None,
       35 +        delay: float = 0.0,
       36 +    ) -> None:
       37 +        self.clip = clip
       38 +        self.fails = fails
       39 +        self.gate = gate
       40 +        self.delay = delay
       41 +        self.calls: list[str] = []
       42 +
       43 +    async def synthesize(self, text: str, out_path: Path, voic
          +e: str | None = None) -> None:
       44 +        self.calls.append(text)
       45 +        if self.gate is not None:
       46 +            await asyncio.to_thread(self.gate.wait, 30)
       47 +        if self.delay:
       48 +            await asyncio.sleep(self.delay)
       49 +        if self.fails(text):
       50 +            raise ConnectionError("fake edge failure")
       51 +        shutil.copyfile(self.clip, out_path)

       90 +def wait_for(predicate: Callable[[], bool], timeout: float = 3
          +0.0, step: float = 0.02) -> None:
       91 +    deadline = time.monotonic() + timeout
       92 +    while time.monotonic() < deadline:

       94 +            return

  file changes it restarts, and the startup wipe (D18) deletes
  backend\storage\jobs. That killed one of my test jobs mid-chapter. I left it
  running and used port 8001 with a separate STORAGE_DIR, so the results below

  Step 0: spike wrap-up

  - docs/spike_results.md: your Render results next to the local Windows and
    local Docker numbers, the D26/D27 conclusions, and the 39-minute estimate.
  - Spike removed: backend/spike/, its mount and spike_enabled are gone. Your
    audiobook-spike service on Render can be deleted if you haven't already.
  - Dockerfile: ARG INCLUDE_PIPER=false, so the voices are only downloaded when
    it's true. I built it locally: it starts and reports tts_fallback: none, and
    the image is 356 MB instead of 472 MB.

  Step 1: D26/D27

  - New setting: TTS_FALLBACK=piper|none. Backoff is 1, 2, 4, 8 s, capped at 8.
  - Edge-only mode: the engine raises NarrationUnavailable after 5 consecutive
    failed chunks. The narrator then fails the current and all remaining
    chapters and stops.
  - Any failed chunk fails its chapter. The chapter's other chunks are cancelled
    and its partial audio is deleted.
  - narrate.py --engine edge now means edge with retries and no fallback. The
    default comes from TTS_FALLBACK. Tested with --fail-edge 100: chapter 1
    failed, the stop triggered in chapter 2, and chapters 3–7 failed without
    calling edge.

  Steps 2–4: API, jobs, cleanup, tests

  All the files listed in the prompt are created. main.py now has
  create_app(settings, engine_factory), so tests can inject a fake engine.

  - Range support: Starlette 1.8.0's FileResponse already handles Range, so I
    used it; a test covers both a full request and bytes=1000-1999.
  - Bug fixed in validate.py: after a corrupt PDF, the exception's traceback
    held the file open, so Windows couldn't delete the rejected upload's folder.
    CORRUPT_FILE is now raised outside the except block.
  - Bug fixed in the filename sanitiser: tabs and newlines are now turned into _
    before control characters are stripped. Before, they were dropped.

  The first health-latency run reported 2,032 ms. That was the client, not the
  server: on Windows, localhost tries IPv6 first and falls back after about 2 s
  on each new connection. The pinger now opens its connection with one untimed
  request first.

  In the Docker run the container's memory peak was 474 MB, but that figure
  includes reclaimable file cache from the MP3s. Render's Memory graph may also
  look high for that reason.

  Choices that go beyond the spec

  - New error codes not in §12:
    - MISSING_FILE (400) for an upload with no file;
    - CHAPTER_NOT_FOUND (404) for a chapter number out of range;
    - NOT_FOUND / BAD_REQUEST for unknown routes and invalid requests.
    - The full book before done/partial returns CHAPTER_NOT_READY (409) with its
      own message.
  - Chapter objects get an error field (TTS_FAILED when failed). chapters_done
    counts ready plus failed chapters.
  - Content-Length check: I allow 64 KB over 50 MB for the multipart framing.
    The byte count while streaming is exact.
  - SERVER_BUSY is returned when 3 jobs are waiting. The running job isn't
    counted, as §4 says.
  - Validation runs twice: once at upload and again inside run_pipeline in the
    worker. That's about 1.4 s extra on Render for 200 pages.
  - Unexpected crashes: a crash during extraction gives CORRUPT_FILE; a crash
    during synthesis gives TTS_FAILED.
  - When edge is down, in-flight chunks are cancelled as soon as a chunk fails,
    so each failed chapter adds about one to the failure count. About 5 chapters
    fail before the job stops.
  - MAX_ACTIVE_JOBS isn't used: there is always exactly one worker thread.
  - New setting EXPIRED_STUB_MINUTES=60: how long an expired job still answers
    JOB_EXPIRED before it becomes JOB_NOT_FOUND.

      55 +    def retrying(self, what: str, call: Callable[[httpx.Client]
         +, T]) -> T:
      56 +        start = time.monotonic()
      57 +        attempt = 0
      58 +        while True:
      59 +            try:
      60 +                return call(self.client)
      61 +            except RETRYABLE as e:
      62 +                waited = time.monotonic() - start
      63 +                if waited >= GIVE_UP_SECONDS:
      64 +                    raise RuntimeError(f"{what}: still failing
         +after {waited:.0f} s ({type(e).__name__}: {e})") from e
      65 +                delay = BACKOFF[min(attempt, len(BACKOFF) - 1)]
      66 +                attempt += 1
      67 +                self.retries += 1
      68 +                print(f"          ! {what}: {type(e).__name__}:
         + {e or 'connection dropped'}; "
      69 +                      f"retrying on a fresh connection in {dela
         +y} s", flush=True)  # fmt: skip
      70 +                time.sleep(delay)
      71 +                self._fresh()
      72 +
      73 +    def get(self, path: str, **kwargs) -> httpx.Response:  # no
         +qa: ANN003
      74 +        return self.retrying(f"GET {path}", lambda c: c.get(pat
         +h, **kwargs))
      75 +
      76 +    def download(self, path: str, target: Path, headers: dict |
         + None = None) -> httpx.Response:
      77 +        def fetch(c: httpx.Client) -> httpx.Response:
      78 +            with c.stream("GET", path, headers=headers or {}, t
         +imeout=600) as r:
      79 +                if r.status_code >= 400:
      80 +                    r.read()
      81 +                    return r
      82 +                with target.open("wb") as fh:  # rewritten from
         + the start on a retry
      83 +                    for piece in r.iter_bytes():
      84 +                        fh.write(piece)
      85 +                return r
      86 +
      87 +        r = self.retrying(f"GET {path}", fetch)
      88 +        if r.status_code >= 400:
      89 +            raise RuntimeError(f"GET {path}: HTTP {r.status_cod
         +e} {r.text}")
      90 +        return r
      91 +

● All four changes are committed and pushed as b04226c, and 214 tests pass. In a
  Docker container limited like Render (0.15 CPU, 512 MB, no swap), document 06
  now narrates 3.5× faster.

  What changed

  1. smoke_api.py:
     - GET requests are retried on a fresh connection when it drops
       (RemoteProtocolError, ConnectError, timeouts), waiting 1, 2, 4, then 8 s,
       and giving up after 2 minutes. The upload (POST) is never retried.
     - If the job comes back 404/410, it says the server probably restarted and
       that jobs aren't restored, then exits with code 2.
     - --resume <job_id> attaches to an existing job, and the job id is printed
       right after upload.
     - Chars/s now counts only the text synthesized while the script was
       watching, so a resumed job reports a correct rate.
  2. Timing logs: one INFO line per chapter, for example:
     Chapter 3 timing: synthesis 18.2 s (67 chunks, 5365 chars), assembly 5.0 s;
     1 ffmpeg + 1 ffprobe calls, 4.8 s in subprocesses
  3. Faster assembly: audio.mp3_info() reads each part's duration and format
     from the MP3 frame headers instead of running ffprobe on every part. It
     skips an ID3 tag and a leading Xing/Info frame, and falls back to ffprobe
     for anything unusual. Each chapter still gets one ffprobe of the joined
     file as a cross-check.
     - Every ffmpeg call now has -threads 1 -nostdin -loglevel error.
     - Tests: durations within 50 ms of ffprobe for silence clips, speech-like
       encodes and joined files, plus the fallback and ID3 cases.
     - On real edge-tts output (3 fresh chunks, 4 Alice chapters, the samples
       file) the difference from ffprobe is exactly 0.000 s. Alice's 12 chapter
       durations are identical before and after.

● Phase 3b is done through step 3, committed and pushed as f5ad4e4, and 218
  tests pass. I tested the whole Docker + Caddy stack locally with 2 CPUs and a
  900 MB limit (like your VM): Alice ran at 616.5 chars/s and memory stayed

  What I couldn't test here: bootstrap.sh and deploy.sh have only been

  HTTP.

  Step 1: code

  - Already in b04226c (last round): the smoke script retries, the per-chapter
    timing logs, faster assembly and -threads 1. This commit adds:
    - Smoke script: a 404 or 410 now prints "Job disappeared - server restarted?
      …". ReadError was already in the retry list.
    - Real edge chunks in the tests: two short real edge-tts clips are saved as
      test fixtures (23 KB and 39 KB) with a .gitignore exception, so the test
      runs offline. The parsed duration matches ffprobe within 50 ms, alone and
      joined with silence.
    - Why count frames, not file size: each frame is exactly 24 ms of audio.
      Using file size instead would be thrown off by the ID3 tag and Xing header
      that ffmpeg puts in joined files. On real edge output the difference from
      ffprobe was 0.000 s.
  - Memory log: every 30 s while a job runs (MEMORY_LOG_INTERVAL_SECONDS), the
    app logs Job … memory: process RSS …, cgroup memory.current …, anon …, plus
    one line at the end of the job. The new code is in app/jobs/memory.py and
    uses only the standard library.

  minutes. The VM's own numbers will confirm this.

  Step 2: deploy/

  - docker-compose.yml:
    - api isn't published on the host, has a health check and a 900 MB limit
      with no swap, keeps jobs in a named volume, and rotates its logs.
    - caddy is pinned to caddy:2.11.7 and serves ports 80/443.
  - Caddyfile: HTTPS for {$API_DOMAIN}, a 55 MB request limit, no compression,
    and long timeouts. It passes caddy validate.
  - .env.example, bootstrap.sh, deploy.sh, README.md: as specified. Both scripts
    are safe to re-run, and bootstrap.sh skips the clone if the folder already
    exists.
  - .gitattributes: keeps LF line endings for the scripts. The raw bootstrap.sh
    on GitHub has no CRLF characters.
  - Local stack test:
    - Uploads through Caddy were handled correctly: 60 MB gave a 413
      FILE_TOO_LARGE in the API's error format, and 52 MB passed the size
      checks.
    - I removed the local stack and the temporary deploy/.env afterwards.
  - Render wording: removed from code, comments and README; the docs keep it as
    history.

  Step 3: what you do

  Your GitHub repo is public, so no token or deploy key is needed.

  On the VM, after your portal setup:
  ssh <user>@<API_DOMAIN>
  curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/lekhyapillarikuppam/Project-2/mai
  n/deploy/bootstrap.sh -o bootstrap.sh && bash bootstrap.sh
  # log out and back in (docker group), then:
  cd ~/Project-2/deploy && nano .env      # set API_DOMAIN
  bash deploy.sh
  - The first build on a 1 GiB VM takes several minutes, using the swap file.
  - deploy.sh waits up to 2 minutes for https://<API_DOMAIN>/api/health, which
    includes getting the certificate. If it times out, it most likely means port
    80 or 443 isn't open in the Azure network security group.

  Then on your laptop (PowerShell), running the second only after the first
  finishes:
  cd C:\dev\Project2\backend; .venv\Scripts\Activate.ps1
  python scripts\smoke_api.py --base https://<API_DOMAIN> --file
  ..\testset\docs\06_no_styles.docx
  python scripts\smoke_api.py --base https://<API_DOMAIN> --file
  ..\testset\docs\01_novel.pdf

  On the VM during the Alice run, run docker stats --no-stream a few times.
  Afterwards, run this:
  cd ~/Project-2/deploy && docker compose logs api | grep -E "timing|memory|done
  in|WARNING|ERROR"

  Paste all of it back. I'll write the "Azure VM" section of
  docs/spike_results.md, and decide on the optional Piper re-test from the
  memory numbers.

  Docker Desktop is still running on your laptop, since I started it for these
  tests.

  - Chapter 1 takes longer to assemble (1.3–1.8 s) because each job makes its
    silence clips once, on its first chapter.
  - Speed compared with Render: 172 chars/s on Render, 599–720 chars/s on the
    VM.
  - Largest allowed book: 100,000 words is about 537,000 characters. That takes
    about 12½ minutes at Alice's speed, or about 15 minutes at the DOCX's speed.
    Memory doesn't grow with book length.
  - Real memory limit: docker stats shows 837 MiB, not 900 MiB, because that's
    all the RAM the 1 GiB VM has free. The container also can't use the host's
    swap file, so 837 MiB is the true ceiling.

  Optional Piper re-test (needs you on the VM)

        1 -# AI-Powered Audiobook Generator
        1 +# Readaloud — AI-Powered Audiobook Generator
        2
        3 -Upload a PDF or DOCX and get a chaptered audiobook you can pla
          -y in the browser (with speed control) or download chapter-by-c
          -hapter or as one MP3.
        3 +Readaloud turns a PDF or Word document into a chaptered audiob
          +ook. It recovers the document's
        4 +structure (chapters and sections) from layout, styles and text
          + patterns, removes what should not be
        5 +read aloud (page numbers, running headers, captions, footnotes
          +, tables, equations), narrates each
        6 +chapter with a neural voice, and lets you start listening to c
          +hapter 1 while the rest is still being
        7 +narrated. Play it in the browser at 0.5×–2× speed, or download
          + single chapters or the whole book as
        8 +MP3. No account, no paid APIs.

          +p.azure.com/api/health>

        9 -## Live demo
       10 -_Coming in Phase 5._
       15 +![Readaloud playing the sample audiobook](docs/screenshots/des
          +ktop-light-sample.png)
       16
       17  ## Features (v1)
       13 -- PDF and DOCX up to 200 pages
       14 -- Automatic cleanup: removes page numbers, running headers/foo
          -ters, broken hyphenation; skips tables, captions, footnotes, e
          -quations
       15 -- Chapter/section detection (built-in outline → heading styles
          - → typography → text patterns → fallback)
       16 -- Neural narration (edge-tts, with local Piper fallback)
       17 -- Chapter-by-chapter playback while the rest is still generati
          -ng
       18 -- 0.5×–2× speed, chapter navigation, auto-advance
       19 -- Download any chapter or the full book
       20 -- Audio auto-deletes after 24 hours
       18 +- PDF (up to 200 pages) and DOCX (up to 100,000 words), up to
          +50 MB; clear messages for scanned,
       19 +  encrypted, corrupt, legacy `.doc` and other files.
       20 +- Clean narration: page numbers, running headers/footers, brok
          +en hyphenation, figure/table captions,
       21 +  footnotes, tables and equations are not read aloud. Two-colu
          +mn pages are read column by column.
       22 +- Chapter detection: built-in PDF outline → Word heading style
          +s → typography → text patterns →
       23 +  fallback parts; contents pages and back matter (references,
          +index) are dropped.
       24 +- Neural narration with edge-tts (4 chunks in parallel), with
          +retries; a chapter that cannot be
       25 +  narrated completely is marked failed instead of silently ski
          +pping text.
       26 +- Chapters become playable one by one; the player auto-advance
          +s and waits for the next chapter if it
       27 +  isn't ready yet.
       28 +- Player: play/pause, ±15 s, seek, previous/next chapter, spee
          +d 0.5×–2× (pitch preserved), keyboard
       29 +  shortcuts, lock-screen controls; it remembers your chapter,
          +position and speed.
       30 +- Downloads: any chapter or the full book as one MP3 with read
          +able filenames.
       31 +- A pre-generated sample audiobook that works even when the ba
          +ckend is down.
       32 +- Temporary by design: audio is deleted within 24 hours; uploa
          +ds are limited to 10 per IP per hour.
       33
       22 -## Tech stack
       34 +## How it works
       35 +
       36 +```mermaid
       37 +flowchart LR
       38 +    U[Upload PDF / DOCX] --> V[Validate<br/>type, signature, s
          +ize,<br/>pages, encryption, scanned]
       39 +    V --> X[Extract<br/>PyMuPDF / python-docx]
       40 +    X --> C[Clean<br/>headers, footers, hyphenation,<br/>capti
          +ons, footnotes, equations]
       41 +    C --> S[Structure detection<br/>outline → styles → typogra
          +phy<br/>→ patterns → fallback]
       42 +    S --> N[Text normalization<br/>numbers, abbreviations, sym
          +bols]
       43 +    N --> K[Sentence chunking<br/>≤ 1,500 chars]
       44 +    K --> T[edge-tts neural voice<br/>4 chunks in parallel]
       45 +    T --> A[ffmpeg assembly<br/>concat without re-encoding]
       46 +    A --> M[Chapter MP3s]
       47 +    M --> P[Browser player<br/>stream with HTTP Range]
       48 +```
       49 +
       50 +Each chapter is narrated and assembled before the next one sta
          +rts, so chapter 1 can be played while
       51 +later chapters are still in progress. Audio is always streamed
          + to files in the job folder, never held
       52 +in memory.
       53 +
       54 +```mermaid
       55 +flowchart LR
       56 +    B[Browser] -->|static site| VC[Vercel<br/>React + Vite bui
          +ld]
       57 +    B -->|HTTPS /api| CD[Caddy<br/>Let's Encrypt TLS]
       58 +    subgraph VM[Azure B2ats v2 VM · Docker Compose]
       59 +        CD --> API[FastAPI container<br/>job worker · ffmpeg ·
          + edge-tts]
       60 +        API --> ST[(jobs volume<br/>temporary audio)]
       61 +    end
       62 +    API -->|neural TTS| E[Microsoft edge-tts]
       63 +```
       64 +
       65 +## Evaluation
       66 +
       67 +Chapter detection is measured on hand-labelled documents (SPEC
          + §13):
       68 +
       69 +| Set | Documents | Chapter F1 | Rejects | Noise paragraphs |
       70 +|---|---|---|---|---|
       71 +| Development set (`testset/`, rules were developed on it) | 1
          +0 | **1.00** (60/60) | 3/3 | 0 |
       72 +| Held-out set, **first run** (`testset_heldout/`, reported nu
          +mber) | 9 | **0.99** (78 of 79, precision 0.99, recall 0.99) |
          + 4/4 | 0 |
       73 +| Held-out set, later runs (after a fix, 2026-10-16 and 2026-1
          +0-18) | 9 | 1.00 (79/79) | 4/4 | 0 |
       74 +
       75 +How the matching works: detected and labelled titles are norma
          +lized (lower-case, chapter labels and
       76 +section numbers stripped, punctuation removed) and compared wi
          +th `rapidfuzz.fuzz.ratio`. Pairs scoring
       77 +at least 80 are matched one-to-one, best pair first, and preci
          +sion, recall and F1 are micro-averaged
       78 +over all documents. A separate check counts leaked page number
          +s or headers ("noise paragraphs").
       79 +
       80 +Why a held-out set (D25): the cleaning and structure rules wer
          +e tuned while looking at the development
       81 +documents, so their score is optimistic. The held-out document
          +s were made with different tools and
       82 +never opened during development; their **first** score (0.99)
          +is the honest number. Later runs are kept
       83 +in [`testset_heldout/results_history.md`](testset_heldout/resu
          +lts_history.md) so it stays clear which
       84 +number is truly held out.
       85 +
       86 +## Performance
       87 +
       88 +| | Render free (first host) | Azure B2ats v2 (current) |
       89 +|---|---|---|
       90 +| CPU / RAM | 0.15 CPU, 512 MB, sleeps after 15 min | 2 vCPU,
          +1 GiB, always on |
       91 +| Narration speed (edge-tts, 4 in parallel) | 172 chars/s | **
          +599–720 chars/s** |
       92 +| *Alice in Wonderland* (12 chapters, 2 h 36 min of audio) | –
          + | narrated in **3 min 16 s** |
       93 +| Largest allowed book (100,000 words ≈ 537,000 chars) | ≈ 52
          +min | **≈ 12–15 min** |
       94 +| Peak memory of the app process | ≈ 100 MB | **131 MB** |
       95 +| Assembly per chapter (ffmpeg concat) | 5–11 s | 0.4–0.5 s |
       96 +
       97 +The local Piper voice was measured too: it needs about 700 MB
          +of the VM's 837 MB together with the
       98 +app (and was killed at 512 MB on Render), so the server uses e
          +dge-tts only (D26). Details:
       99 +[`docs/spike_results.md`](docs/spike_results.md).
      100 +
      101 +## Limits and non-goals
      102 +- Limits: `.pdf`/`.docx` only, 50 MB, 200 PDF pages, 100,000 w
          +ords; one job at a time with up to 3
      103 +  waiting; 10 uploads per IP per hour; audio kept at most 24 h
          + (a server restart also clears it).
      104 +- Not supported in v1: accounts or saved libraries, OCR / scan
          +ned PDFs, `.doc`/`.txt`/`.epub`,
      105 +  languages other than English, reading tables/captions/footno
          +tes/equations, multiple voices, paid APIs.
      106 +
      107 +## Tech stack and layout
      108 +
      109  | Part | Tech |
      110  |---|---|
       25 -| Backend | Python, FastAPI, PyMuPDF, python-docx, edge-tts, P
          -iper, ffmpeg |
       26 -| Frontend | React + Vite |
       27 -| Hosting | Azure VM with Docker + Caddy (backend), Vercel (fr
          -ontend) |
      111 +| Backend | Python 3.11, FastAPI, PyMuPDF, python-docx, rapidf
          +uzz, edge-tts, Piper (local only), ffmpeg |
      112 +| Frontend | React 19 + Vite, react-router-dom, plain CSS; Vit
          +est + Testing Library |
      113 +| Hosting | Azure VM with Docker Compose + Caddy (backend), Ve
          +rcel (frontend) |

      158 +
      159 +## Key decisions
      160 +
      161 +Full list with reasons: [`docs/DECISIONS.md`](docs/DECISIONS.m
          +d).
      162 +
      163 +| # | Decision | Why |
      164 +|---|---|---|
      165 +| D4/D5 | Only PDF and DOCX; scanned PDFs rejected (no OCR) |
          +Reliable extraction; OCR is slow and error-prone |
      166 +| D6 | 200 pages / 100,000 words / 50 MB | Bounded generation
          +time and disk on free hosting |
      167 +| D8 | Tables, captions, footnotes, equations are skipped | Th
          +ey don't make sense spoken |
      168 +| D13 | Chapters generated in order, playable as soon as ready
          + | Long books take minutes; start listening at once |
      169 +| D16/D25 | Labelled test set with F1; a held-out set never in
          +spected | A measurable, honest result |
      170 +| D18/D19 | Jobs are temporary and not restored after a restar
          +t; the UI never promises a time | No paid storage |
      171 +| D26 | Server narrates with edge-tts only, with patient retri
          +es | Piper does not fit in the VM's memory next to the app |
      172 +| D27 | A chunk that fails fails its whole chapter | A listene
          +r must never miss text without being told |
      173 +| D28 | Backend on an Azure VM behind Caddy, not Render free |
          + ~4× faster narration, always on |
      174 +| D30 | 10 uploads per IP per hour | Public site on student cr
          +edit |
      175 +
      176 +## Future work
      177 +- **v2: one voice per character** — detect dialogue, attribute
          + each line to a speaker with a free LLM
      178 +  (Gemini or Groq), merge character aliases, map characters to
          + distinct edge-tts voices, a v1/v2
      179 +  narration toggle and cast list in the UI, and measure speake
          +r-attribution accuracy on a labelled
      180 +  chapter (SPEC §15).
      181 +- OCR for scanned PDFs, more input formats (EPUB), and more la
          +nguages.
      182 +- Persistent storage so jobs survive a restart, and more than
          +one worker for more users.

● Phase 5 is done through step 5 and pushed as 5ec3769. I'm now waiting for your
  Vercel URL and the production check results (step D below).

  What changed

  - Per-IP upload limit (D30):
    - Each client IP gets 10 uploads per rolling hour. Over the limit, the
      server answers 429 RATE_LIMITED with Retry-After, without reading the
      file.
    - Rejected uploads count too. UPLOADS_PER_IP_PER_HOUR=10 turns it off with
      0.
    - The client IP comes from X-Forwarded-For only when the request comes from
      a private Docker-network address (Caddy); otherwise the direct address is
      used. Python's built-in "private" check also covers documentation ranges
      like 198.51.100.x, so I listed Docker's private ranges explicitly.
    - The code is in backend/app/api/ratelimit.py, with 20 tests in
      tests/test_ratelimit.py.
    - The other API tests run with the limit off, since they all share one test
      address.
    - The frontend shows the SPEC §12 message; one new test covers it.
  - Docs:
    - a new root README.md with the pitch, two Mermaid diagrams, evaluation,
      performance, run/deploy notes and key decisions;
    - docs/VIVA.md with a 2-minute summary, "where is the AI", 20 questions,
      limitations and a demo script;
    - docs/DEMO_VIDEO.md with a 10-shot list;
    - CLAUDE.md gained dev:remote, the frontend test/build commands and the
      evaluation commands.
    - <VERCEL_URL> is still a placeholder in the README and VIVA files.
  - Also in the commit: your SPEC revision 7, the D30 entry, and phase5.md.

      298 +_CANDIDATE_KEY = re.compile(r"^c(\d+):(\d+):(\d+)$")
      299 +
      300 +
      301 +def _dropped_quotes(
      302 +    chapter: str,
      303 +    paragraphs: list[str],
      304 +    gold_by_paragraph: dict[int, list[dict[str, Any]]],
      305 +    items: list[dict[str, Any]],
      306 +    marks: str,
      307 +    family: str,
      308 +) -> list[Finding]:
      309 +    """Narrowed candidates: quote marks of the family left in
          +the original candidate span that no
      310 +    gold span or Not-a-quote decision covers (a quote lost whe
          +n the span was narrowed)."""
      311 +    covered: dict[int, list[tuple[int, int]]] = {}
      312 +    for p, row in gold_by_paragraph.items():
      313 +        covered.setdefault(p, []).extend((x["start"], x["end"]
          +) for x in row)
      314 +    for it in items:
      315 +        if it["decision"] == "not_quote":
      316 +            covered.setdefault(it["p"], []).append((it["s"], i
          +t["e"]))
      317 +
      318 +    findings: list[Finding] = []
      319 +    for it in items:
      320 +        m = _CANDIDATE_KEY.match(str(it.get("key", "")))
      321 +        if it.get("origin") != "candidate" or not m:
      322 +            continue
      323 +        p, s0, e0 = (int(g) for g in m.groups())
      324 +        if (it["s"], it["e"]) == (s0, e0) or not 0 <= p < len(
          +paragraphs):
      325 +            continue
      326 +        text = paragraphs[p]
      327 +        loose = [
      328 +            i for i in range(s0, min(e0, len(text)))
      329 +            if text[i] in marks
      330 +            and not (family == "single" and _is_apostrophe(tex
          +t, i))
      331 +            and not any(s <= i < e for s, e in covered.get(p,
          +[]))
      332 +        ]  # fmt: skip
      333 +        if loose:
      334 +            s, e = loose[0], loose[-1] + 1
      335 +            findings.append(Finding("warn", chapter, p, s, e,
          +_excerpt(text[s:e]),
      336 +                                    f"possible dropped quote:
          +{len(loose)} quote mark(s) of the narrowed candidate "
      337 +                                    f"[{s0}:{e0}] not covered
          +by a gold span or Not a quote"))  # fmt: skip
      338 +    return findings
      339 +

  What changed
  - backend/scripts/label_dialogue.py: a new warning, "possible dropped quote".
    For each candidate whose span was narrowed, it looks at the original span.
    It reports any quote marks of the chapter's style there that aren't inside a
    gold span or a "Not a quote" decision. Apostrophes are ignored. It gives
    one warning per candidate, covering the uncovered marks.
  - backend/tests/test_label_lint.py: one new test. It checks that a narrowed
    candidate with a dropped quote warns, and that it stops warning once the
    rest is labelled as a quote or as "Not a quote".

  Tests: the lint tests pass (12), and so do all 58 dialogue, segment and label
  tests. The full pytest -q run went over the 2-minute limit and is still

  without waiting for it.

      185
      186 +# --- attribution (SPEC §15.9, D39) --------------------------
          +-------------------------------------
      187 +
      188 +TIE_POINTS = 0.02  # D39: candidates within 2 percentage point
          +s of the best voice accuracy tie
      189 +
      190 +
      191 +@dataclass(frozen=True)
      192 +class Candidate:
      193 +    method: Method
      194 +    model: str | None  # None for the baseline
      195 +
      196 +    @property
      197 +    def name(self) -> str:
      198 +        return "baseline" if self.model is None else f"{self.m
          +ethod} · {self.model}"
      199 +
      200 +
      201 +@dataclass
      202 +class AttributionScore:
      203 +    utterances: int = 0  # gold utterances matched by detectio
          +n (the scored ones)
      204 +    missed: int = 0  # gold utterances detection missed entire
          +ly
      205 +    correct: int = 0
      206 +    explicit: int = 0
      207 +    explicit_correct: int = 0
      208 +    implicit: int = 0
      209 +    implicit_correct: int = 0
      210 +    unknown_predicted: int = 0
      211 +    voice_correct: int = 0
      212 +    errors: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
      213 +
      214 +    def add(self, other: "AttributionScore") -> None:
      215 +        for name in self.__dataclass_fields__:
      216 +            if name != "errors":
      217 +                setattr(self, name, getattr(self, name) + geta
          +ttr(other, name))
      218 +
      219 +
      220 +@dataclass
      221 +class CandidateResult:
      222 +    candidate: Candidate
      223 +    per_chapter: dict[str, AttributionScore] = field(default_f
          +actory=dict)
      224 +    usage: list[UsageRecord] = field(default_factory=list)
      225 +    windows_total: int = 0
      226 +    windows_baseline: int = 0  # windows that fell back to the
          + baseline (D34)
      227 +
      228 +    @property
      229 +    def total(self) -> AttributionScore:
      230 +        t = AttributionScore()
      231 +        for s in self.per_chapter.values():
      232 +            t.add(s)
      233 +        return t
      234 +
      235 +    @property
      236 +    def voice_accuracy(self) -> float:
      237 +        t = self.total
      238 +        return t.voice_correct / t.utterances if t.utterances
          +else 0.0
      239 +
      240 +    @property
      241 +    def tokens(self) -> int:
      242 +        return sum(u.prompt_tokens + u.completion_tokens for u
          + in self.usage)
      243 +
      244 +    @property
      245 +    def median_latency(self) -> float:
      246 +        return statistics.median(u.latency_seconds for u in se
          +lf.usage) if self.usage else 0.0
      247 +
      248 +
      249 +def _ratio(a: int, b: int) -> str:
      250 +    return f"{a / b:.2f}" if b else "n/a"
      251 +
      252 +
      253 +def load_gold_cast(name: str) -> dict[str, dict]:
      254 +    path = DIALOGUE_DIR / "labels" / f"{name}.cast.json"
      255 +    return json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")) if pat
          +h.exists() else {}
      256 +
      257 +
      258 +def score_attribution(
      259 +    r: ChapterResult, gold_cast: dict[str, dict], attributions
          +: dict[str, Attribution], cast: Cast,
      260 +    casting: VoiceCasting,
      261 +) -> AttributionScore:  # fmt: skip
      262 +    """Score one chapter's attributions on the gold utterances
          + detection matched."""
      263 +    gold_keys = {c: {normalize_name(n) for n in [c, *info.get(
          +"aliases", [])]} for c, info in gold_cast.items()}
      264 +    reverse = {g: i for i, g in r.matched.items()}
      265 +    utterances: dict[str, list[int]] = defaultdict(list)
      266 +    for g, line in enumerate(r.lines):
      267 +        utterances[line["utterance"]].append(g)
      268 +    score = AttributionScore()
      269 +    for indices in utterances.values():
      270 +        first = r.lines[indices[0]]
      271 +        hit = next((g for g in indices if g in reverse), None)
      272 +        if hit is None:
      273 +            score.missed += 1
      274 +            continue
      275 +        a = attributions[r.pred_utt[reverse[hit]]]
      276 +        gold = first["speaker"]
      277 +        member = cast.resolve(a.speaker) if a.speaker != UNKNO
          +WN else None
      278 +        predicted_keys = member.keys() if member else ({normal
          +ize_name(a.speaker)} if a.speaker != UNKNOWN else set())
      279 +        keys = gold_keys.get(gold, {normalize_name(gold)})
      280 +        correct = a.speaker == UNKNOWN if gold == UNKNOWN else
          + bool(predicted_keys & keys)
      281 +        if gold == UNKNOWN:
      282 +            expected = casting.narrator
      283 +        else:
      284 +            gold_member = next((m for m in cast.members if m.k
          +eys() & keys), None)
      285 +            gender = gold_cast.get(gold, {}).get("gender", "un
          +known")
      286 +            expected = casting.voice_for(gold_member) if gold_
          +member else casting.minor_voice(gender)
      287 +        heard = casting.voice_for(member)
      288 +        explicit = any(r.lines[g]["explicit"] for g in indices
          +)
      289 +        score.utterances += 1
      290 +        score.correct += correct
      291 +        score.explicit += explicit
      292 +        score.explicit_correct += explicit and correct
      293 +        score.implicit += not explicit
      294 +        score.implicit_correct += (not explicit) and correct
      295 +        score.unknown_predicted += a.speaker == UNKNOWN
      296 +        score.voice_correct += heard == expected
      297 +        if not correct or heard != expected:
      298 +            span = (first["paragraph"], first["start"], first[
          +"end"])
      299 +            shown = member.name if member else a.speaker
      300 +            score.errors.append(
      301 +                f"¶{span[0]} `{excerpt(r.paragraphs, span)}` g
          +old **{gold}** → predicted **{shown}** "
      302 +                f"({a.method}/{a.reason}); voice expected {exp
          +ected.label}, heard {heard.label}"
      303 +            )
      304 +    return score
      305 +
      306 +
      307 +async def evaluate_attribution(
      308 +    results: list[ChapterResult], methods: list[Method], model
          +s: list[str]
      309 +) -> list[CandidateResult]:
      310 +    """Every candidate on every chapter; each LLM model runs o
          +nce per chapter (both LLM methods
      311 +    share its answers)."""
      312 +    settings = get_settings()
      313 +    candidates = [Candidate("baseline", None)] if "baseline" i
          +n methods else []
      314 +    candidates += [Candidate(m, model) for model in models for
          + m in methods if m != "baseline"]
      315 +    out = {c: CandidateResult(c) for c in candidates}
      316 +    llm_methods = [m for m in methods if m != "baseline"]
      317 +    for r in results:
      318 +        gold_cast = load_gold_cast(r.name)
      319 +        baseline = baseline_attribute(r.paragraphs, r.spans)
      320 +        ranking, genders = rank_texts([r.paragraphs], r.family
          +)
      321 +        if "baseline" in methods:
      322 +            casting = new_casting(settings.edge_voice, setting
          +s.max_cast)
      323 +            cast = cast_with_voices(casting, ranking, genders,
          + [])
      324 +            c = Candidate("baseline", None)
      325 +            out[c].per_chapter[r.name] = score_attribution(r,
          +gold_cast, baseline.attributions, cast, casting)
      326 +        if not llm_methods:
      327 +            continue
      328 +        if not settings.groq_api_key:
      329 +            raise SystemExit("GROQ_API_KEY is not set: LLM met
          +hods cannot run")
      330 +        for model in models:
      331 +            limiter = TokenLimiter(settings.llm_tokens_per_min
          +ute, settings.llm_max_requests_per_day)
      332 +            client = GroqClient(settings.groq_api_key, model,
          +limiter, cache_dir=settings.llm_cache_path)
      333 +            roster = Roster()
      334 +            llm = await llm_attribute(r.paragraphs, r.spans, c
          +lient, roster, baseline, settings.llm_window_words)
      335 +            for method in llm_methods:
      336 +                combined = combine(method, baseline, llm)
      337 +                casting = new_casting(settings.edge_voice, set
          +tings.max_cast)
      338 +                cast = cast_with_voices(casting, ranking, gend
          +ers, roster.reports(), roster.distinct)
      339 +                result = out[Candidate(method, model)]
      340 +                result.per_chapter[r.name] = score_attribution
          +(r, gold_cast, combined.attributions, cast, casting)
      341 +                result.usage += client.usage
      342 +                result.windows_total += llm.windows_total
      343 +                result.windows_baseline += llm.windows_total -
          + llm.windows_llm
      344 +    return list(out.values())
      345 +
      346 +
      347 +def select_d39(results: list[CandidateResult]) -> tuple[Candid
          +ateResult, list[CandidateResult]]:
      348 +    """D39: highest micro voice accuracy; candidates within 2
          +points tie; ties broken by fewer total
      349 +    tokens, then lower median latency, then `llm_with_baseline
          +_override` over `llm`."""
      350 +    best = max(c.voice_accuracy for c in results)
      351 +    ties = [c for c in results if best - c.voice_accuracy <= T
          +IE_POINTS + 1e-9]
      352 +    ties.sort(key=lambda c: (c.tokens, c.median_latency, c.can
          +didate.method != "llm_with_baseline_override"))
      353 +    return ties[0], ties
      354 +
      355 +
      356 +def render_attribution(results: list[CandidateResult], chapter
          +s: list[str]) -> list[str]:
      357 +    chosen, ties = select_d39(results)
      358 +    out = [
      359 +        "",
      360 +        "## Attribution candidates (D39)",
      361 +        "",
      362 +        "Scored on gold utterances matched by detection. Voice
          + accuracy uses the system's own voice map "
      363 +        "(each chapter ranked as a whole book). Tokens and lat
          +ency are those of the original Groq requests.",
      364 +        "",
      365 +        "| Candidate | Voice acc (micro) | " + " | ".join(f"Vo
          +ice {n}" for n in chapters)
      366 +        + " | Attribution acc | Explicit | Implicit | % UNKNOW
          +N | Requests | Prompt tok | Completion tok | "
      367 +        "Median latency (s) | Total latency (s) | Baseline win
          +dows |",
      368 +        "|---" * (12 + len(chapters)) + "|",
      369 +    ]
      370 +    for c in results:
      371 +        t = c.total
      372 +        per = " | ".join(_ratio(c.per_chapter[n].voice_correct
          +, c.per_chapter[n].utterances) for n in chapters)
      373 +        prompt = sum(u.prompt_tokens for u in c.usage)
      374 +        completion = sum(u.completion_tokens for u in c.usage)
      375 +        latency = sum(u.latency_seconds for u in c.usage)
      376 +        fallback = f"{c.windows_baseline}/{c.windows_total}" i
          +f c.candidate.model else "-"
      377 +        out.append(
      378 +            f"| {c.candidate.name} | {_ratio(t.voice_correct,
          +t.utterances)} | {per} | {_ratio(t.correct, t.utterances)} | "
      379 +            f"{_ratio(t.explicit_correct, t.explicit)} | {_rat
          +io(t.implicit_correct, t.implicit)} | "
      380 +            f"{100 * t.unknown_predicted / t.utterances if t.u
          +tterances else 0:.0f}% | {len(c.usage)} | {prompt} | "
      381 +            f"{completion} | {c.median_latency:.2f} | {latency
          +:.1f} | {fallback} |"
      382 +        )
      383 +    missed = ", ".join(f"{n} {results[0].per_chapter[n].missed
          +}" for n in chapters)
      384 +    out += [
      385 +        "",
      386 +        f"Gold utterances missed by detection (not scored): {m
          +issed}.",
      387 +        "",
      388 +        f"**D39 selection: {chosen.candidate.name}** (voice ac
          +curacy {chosen.voice_accuracy:.3f}; tied within "
      389 +        f"2 points: {', '.join(c.candidate.name for c in ties)
          +}).",
      390 +    ]
      391 +    for n in chapters:
      392 +        out += ["", f"## {n}: attribution errors ({chosen.cand
          +idate.name})", ""]
      393 +        out += [f"- {e}" for e in chosen.per_chapter[n].errors
          +] or ["None."]
      394 +    return out

● Phase 6.2 report

  Phase 6.2 is done, committed as 5baeea6 and pushed to main. All 358 backend
  tests pass, and D39 picked llm with qwen/qwen3.8-27b (dev voice accuracy
  0.99). Nothing was run on ball_ch03. I stopped here and did not start 6.3.

  Gate

  - Lint: 0 errors on both dev chapters. I didn't run it on ball_ch03; your
    amendment says it is clean.
  - Detection: F1 is 0.99 on Alice and 1.00 on Brown.
  - Grouping: Alice went from 0.76 to 0.77, Brown is 0.98. Grouping stays below
    0.9 because the gold labels split many same-speaker broken quotes (‘I do,’
    Alice hastily replied; ‘at least…’) into separate utterances, and the
    segmenter joins them. These pairs have the same speaker, so they are not
    over-joins under your definition.
  - Over-joining (different gold speakers in one predicted utterance): Alice had
    2 of 44 predicted multi-span utterances before the fix and 1 of 43 (2.3%)
    after. Brown has 0 of 50. Both are under the 5% limit.
    - Fixed, ¶76: ’Sh! sh! (gold UNKNOWN) was joined with the Dormouse's quote.
      New general rule in segment.py (rule 4): narration between two quotes that
      starts with and / but / while plus a newly named speaker (and the
      Dormouse sulkily remarked,) starts a new utterance. Pronouns don't trigger
      it (and she added stays one speaker). A test was added.
    - Left, ¶22: this looks like a label problem, which I did not edit. Q36 ‘you
      shouldn’t have put it in with the bread-knife.’ is labelled the March
      Hare, but the text says "the Hatter grumbled: ‘you shouldn’t…’". All
      methods predict Hatter.
  - Under-joining (remaining, doesn't block): Brown ¶47 (Dr Hood, 3 spans) and
    ¶68 (Father Brown, 3 spans). In each, the first span is split from the next
    two.

  Groq parameters (checked with real requests)

  - gpt-oss-120b: reasoning_effort: "low" works: 895 completion tokens on Alice
    window 1, of which 519 are reasoning. "none" is rejected with HTTP 400
    ("must be one of low, medium, or high"), so thinking can't be turned off.
  - qwen/qwen3.8-27b: the parameter is accepted but changes nothing. On the same
    Alice window (56 quotes, 2,364 prompt tokens), completion tokens are 652
    with thinking on (default), 652 with it off (none), and 652 with no
    parameter, and there is never any reasoning output. The model doesn't appear
    to think on Groq, so the tokens can't drop. I kept none so the setting is
    explicit.
  - Rate limits: qwen has a separate output limit of 1,000 tokens per minute on
    this key, which allows about one window per minute. The client handles this
    through 429 and retry-after; the runs hit 4 short waits and no window fell
    back to the baseline. The headers report 8,000 tokens per minute and 1,000
    requests per day.
  - Both model ids are still listed on /models.

  Candidates (dev, micro)
